Some of us have a go-to color in every situation—a color that's always the story we want to tell. 

  
![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/ct/2014/ct_wk19_299_cardart_eureka.jpg)

Art by [Ryan Pancoast](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Search/Default.aspx?output=spoiler&method=visual&action=advanced&artist=%5B%22Ryan+Pancoast%22%5D) 

I'm a bit more mercenary in playing favorites. Depending on the context, I'll play any colors that suit my needs: You can't be too choosey when you're drafting, after all. Even in Commander, I strive to move across the rainbow, constantly trying new commanders in colors I've played the least recently.

For example, I built a deck using *every* color that's wacky and off-the-wall just for playing with some friends I was catching up with at [Grand Prix Minneapolis](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://magic.wizards.com/en/events/coverage/parks-great-scape-minneapolis) last week: 

#### Stybs's Atogatog

#####

A streak of vague "mess around with things opponents do and have" backed up by a little land acceleration, Equipment, and removal. It's loose, wildly different every game, and absolutely a blast for me. Mission accomplished.

But hidden in even this colorful deck are clues toward a greater loyalty. There's one color that gets a little extra attention over the others, and finds its way into more Commander decks than I can keep track of.

The section of my Commander Box with the most cards? Green, by a narrow margin over lands.

There's just something appealing to me about the primal power of playing green in Commander. Green gets some of the best ways to accelerate mana, has some of the biggest and baddest creatures in the game, and finds overlaps with themes as disparate as graveyard recursion, token generation, and using tons of enchantments for fun and profit. What *can't* green do? 

I mean, it can even do this:

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/ct/2014/Eureka.jpg)  

### Bigger, Badder, Bolder

[Eureka](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eureka) is the original fatty maker. While **Magic**'s formative years were where spells—instants, sorceries, and artifacts—ruled the battlefield, [Eureka](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eureka) was a not-so-subtle nod to what green was supposed to be doing: putting gigantic creatures into play. [Force of Nature](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Force+of+Nature) and [Craw Wurm](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Craw+Wurm) were, generally, larger than anything an opponent could cast. 

<table><tbody><tr><td>&#10;						<div><div><img alt="Force of Nature" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Force+of+Nature"></div></div>&#10;					</td>&#10;					<td>&#10;						<div><div><img alt="Craw Wurm" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Craw+Wurm"></div></div>&#10;					</td>&#10;				</tr></tbody></table>

  

Today, there are much more powerful, and expensive, permanents to put into play.

[Eureka](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eureka) is a card that can do some incredible things in a sixty-card deck, and I'm sure cheating an oversized or off-color monstrosity will happen in Vintage Masters draft. Where I believe [Eureka](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eureka) will see the most play is in the multiplayer world of Commander on **Magic Online**. 

Fortunately, there was no shortage sneaky permanent-dumping you're already up to, starting with Joe.

> When it comes to getting things into play, you hit the nail on the head when you mentioned [Primal Surge](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primal+Surge). I challenged myself when *Dragon's Maze* came out and built a [Ruric Thar, the Unbowed](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ruric+Thar%2C+the+Unbowed) deck as a [Primal Surge](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primal+Surge) build. It does exactly what Ruric Thar should do: plays big creatures and beats face just like any good Gruul lord would. It also includes some ways of sneaking guys into play with [Garruk, Caller of Beasts](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk%2C+Caller+of+Beasts); lands into play with [Burgeoning](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burgeoning); and, of course... [Primal Surge](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primal+Surge). Casting this in this deck will spill every permanent in the deck into play, but there is no instant-kill unless I hit things in the right order (hitting [Ogre Battledriver](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ogre+Battledriver) early in the Surge for example). 
> 
> <table><tbody><tr><td>&#10;							<div><div><img alt="Primal Surge" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Primal+Surge"></div></div>&#10;						</td>&#10;					</tr></tbody></table>
> 
> Anyways, here's the deck. I hope you enjoy it. It's proven to be quite fun to play. RURIC THAR SMASH!
> 
> —Joe

Joe's deck has no shortage of things to put into play with [Eureka](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eureka), although the "best" [Primal Surge](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primal+Surge) decks don't include any nonpermanents other than [Primal Surge](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primal+Surge). Alex has a different process using a similar card: [Genesis Wave](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Genesis+Wave). 

> My [Kamahl, Fist of Krosa](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kamahl%2C+Fist+of+Krosa) deck tends to make excellent use of [Genesis Wave](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Genesis+Wave) frequently. My personal favorite card to G-Wave into is [Craterhoof Behemoth](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Craterhoof+Behemoth). Craterhoof tends to hit with plenty of Forests, which allows me to further amp up my board. If I'm particularly lucky I have an overwhelming stampede in my hand, which tends to let me swing for lethal. If I don't get the Forests, or don't have overwhelming stampede, I can usually swing for a ton of damage regardless. If Kamahl's on the field, I can animate my army and further pump up my guys. It's a fun way to surprise-kill opponents.

<table><tbody><tr><td>&#10;						<div><div><img alt="Genesis Wave" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Genesis+Wave"></div></div>&#10;					</td>&#10;				</tr></tbody></table>

—Alex

Sneaking around with [Overrun](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Overrun)-on-a-stick is one way to get some value going, and [Genesis Wave](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Genesis+Wave) is something that always terrifies me when it's cast in a game. Other cards that tip me off that are shenanigans afoot are [Aluren](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aluren), [Hibernation's End](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hibernation%27s+End), and [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair). As this unnamed reader shared, no good comes from such powerful ways to cheat things onto the battlefield: 

> Regarding your request for favorite permanents to sneak on the battlefield: 
> 
> The mad scientist here. When I'm not building Rube Goldberg death machines around [Sliver Queen](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sliver+Queen), I'll make an actual Sliver deck that's green-dominant and likes to accelerate things onto the battlefield. I have four pieces of green tech for this: [Aluren](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aluren), [Hibernation's End](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hibernation%27s+End), [Lurking Predators](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lurking+Predators), and [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair). 
> 
> [Wild Pair](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wild+Pair) is the preferred thing to fetch with tutors, and its favorite target sneaks onto the battlefield when [Sliver Queen](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sliver+Queen) comes onto the battlefield: [Sliver Legion](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sliver+Legion). Thassa and Pharika also pair off nicely, as do Purphoros and Kruphix, if the Gods happen to be active as creatures when they enter the battlefield. 
> 
> <table><tbody><tr><td>&#10;							<div><div><img alt="Wild Pair" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Wild+Pair"></div></div>&#10;						</td>&#10;						<td>&#10;							<div><div><img alt="Sliver Legion" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Sliver+Legion"></div></div>&#10;						</td>&#10;					</tr></tbody></table>
> 
>   
> 
> [Aluren](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Aluren)'s preferred Slivers to drop on the battlefield at instant speed (and for free) are [Crystalline Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crystalline+Sliver), [Necrotic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Necrotic+Sliver), and [Harmonic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Harmonic+Sliver). But a surprise [Mirror Entity](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mirror+Entity) or [Pharika, God of Affliction](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pharika%2C+God+of+Affliction) is also fun. 
> 
> [Hibernation's End](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hibernation%27s+End) is very useful in this deck. Among options not already mentioned for it are: [Virulent Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Virulent+Sliver) at one; [Amoeboid Changeling](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Amoeboid+Changeling) at two (Gods like to be Slivers, too!); [Athreos, God of Passage](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Athreos%2C+God+of+Passage) at three; [Purphoros, God of the Forge](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Purphoros%2C+God+of+the+Forge) at four; [Kruphix, God of Horizons](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kruphix%2C+God+of+Horizons) at five; and [Megantic Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Megantic+Sliver) at six. 
> 
> <table><tbody><tr><td>&#10;							<div><div><img alt="Aluren" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Aluren"></div></div>&#10;						</td>&#10;						<td>&#10;							<div><div><img alt="Hibernation's End" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Hibernation%27s+End"></div></div>&#10;						</td>&#10;					</tr></tbody></table>

Ultimately, [Eureka](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eureka) is just one of many ways playing green means getting mean with permanents. What sets [Eureka](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eureka) apart from so many other cheaty ways to get around mana costs is that it lets everyone in on the action. (Insert [Phelddagrif](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Phelddagrif) and some Group Hug mention here.) 

But for decks planning on a party for permanents, sharing isn't something you should be caring about.

### And Now For Something Completely the Same

While I'm an unabashed fan of Forests in Commander decks, Islands often make me uncomfortable. There're only so many times I can see [Snow-Covered Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snow-Covered+Island)s help [Arcum Dagsson](https://web.archive.org/web/20140515000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arcum+Dagsson) do really powerful things with a few artifacts before the reflex to check out of the game kicks in. 

But since we spent a week looking at my favorite archetype—"cheaty" green decks—it's only fair to flip things on its head: **What is your favorite blue commander for Commander, and what artifacts do you use with it? (And why?)** 

*   Feedback via email
*   300 word limit to explain why you set aside an entire deck
*   **Sample decklist is requested** (does not count against word limit)
*   Decklists should be formatted with one card per line with just a leading number, such as "3 Mountain"—just a space (no "x" or "-") between the number and the card name, without subtotals by card type (Submissions that don't follow this rule will be ignored.)
*   Name and email required (non-personal information to be used in column)

While I can't promise to ever try a busted blue deck myself, sharing how Commander aficionados who love them handle such decks is a bridge we'd have to cross sooner or later.

Join us next week when we unlock the power of a new machine. See you then!
